Part A · Beta programme

What you install, how you get going and what happens to your feedback.

A1What Etmar is — and what it isn’t

So your expectations are right before you cast off.

Etmar is an iOS app for sailors (iPhone and iPad) that brings together a digital logbook, a regatta module with analysis, a trim diary, instruments from the NMEA network, a harbour guide, SOS radio procedure cards, a workshop (projects and winter lay-up planning) and a sailing calendar. It is offline-first — at sea it needs no network —, and it has no account and no server. Your data lives on the device and, if you like, in your private iCloud.

The idea behind it: Put the phone away, sail, look back afterwards. Everything that can happen automatically happens automatically — recording, casting off, arrival, the starting gun. Whatever is operated on deck is designed for one-handed use with wet fingers in a seaway: large buttons, one or two taps, a mis-tap has no consequences.

Important Etmar is no substitute for a nautical chart or a chartplotter — it records what happened. The map display is based on OpenStreetMap: seamarks are community data, and the depth contours come from a model with a grid of around 100 m (EMODnet). Neither is an official source, and neither is corrected from Notices to Mariners. Keep navigating with whatever you have navigated with so far — and test Etmar alongside it.

Deliberately not built: route calculation around land (Go-To is a bearing), social features, public sharing, a server of its own or an account.

↑ Back to contents

A2Setting up TestFlight

Five minutes once, after that updates arrive on their own.

  1. Download TestFlight from the App Store (by Apple, free).
  2. Open the invitation link from your email on the iPhone. TestFlight opens and shows Etmar.
  3. Tap Install, done. The beta sits alongside your normal apps; the TestFlight symbol (orange dot) next to the app name shows that it is a test version.
  4. In TestFlight, under Etmar → switch on Automatic Updates. Then every new build arrives without you doing anything; otherwise TestFlight lets you know with a notification.

iOS 17 or later is required. A build runs for 90 days, after which TestFlight asks for an update — that is normal and not a bug. On first launch you read the note “What to Test”; it always says what is new since the last build. In the app, the short version for each build is under “What’s new” (Settings → Help or Tools → Help & demo). After an update, the line “New in Build …” in the logbook in harbour points it out; a tap opens the list. Without a tap it disappears after a week; underway and after a reinstall it does not appear.

When updating Your data is kept — a new build only replaces the app, not the database. Only if you delete the app is the local data gone. After reinstalling, the onboarding comes back, on a second device too. Its first page is called “Your data”: tap “Continue from iCloud” there. Then you don’t add a boat, you only answer location and cruising area (offline maps don’t come from iCloud), and at the end you close Etmar once via the app switcher and open it again — only then does the app fetch the iCloud copy. Without an iCloud account on the device, “Continue from iCloud” is greyed out, with the reason below it. Up to Build 35 you switch on “Back up to iCloud” at the location step instead; the boat that the onboarding asks for along the way then sits empty next to yours from iCloud and is initially the active one — you switch to yours via the boat name at the top of the logbook or under Tools → Boat, section “Switch boat” (C10). Only there do your voyages appear. If your boat with its content only arrives while such an empty boat is active, the app switches over in the logbook by itself; a notice says so, and the empty one stays until you delete it under Tools → Boat. If both are already there, you switch as just described. For backup, see C16.

After a reinstall, iCloud needs time. Boat, voyages, entries, tracks and images follow — with many voyages, over several minutes. Until the first boat has arrived, the logbook shows “iCloud is loading your boats…”; “Add boat” is only offered there after ten minutes. While the rest is still coming in, the line “iCloud is still loading” appears in the logbook under “All voyages”. So during that time the list is still incomplete, and that is not data loss. Recording, casting off and MOB still work — but you should only start a new voyage once the line has gone, otherwise you end up with a second open voyage next to the synced one.
↑ Back to contents

A3Ready in ten minutes

Look around first, then add your own boat — in that order it is quickest.

1. Start demo mode

Tools → Help & demo → Start demo. First the app asks: Voyage or Regatta. The voyage is the demo as described here; the regatta demo sails the NSV Wednesday race in Neustadt Bay (C5, C16). The app fills up with sample data: demo boat “Vertigo Express”, a voyage Kühlungsborn–Gedser with a stretch under engine and a radio call, an open day sail with a stage plan, a race with a complete analysis, trim entries, calendar events, seamarks and depths of the Bay of Mecklenburg. On top of that comes a season to analyse: five more races over six weeks (one of them without a wind sensor, for the GPS estimate), a voyage Neustadt–Grömitz and a strong-wind training session with a reef and storm jib — so no card under Tools → Analyses stays empty. The app also simulates NMEA data — so you see the instruments too, without onboard electronics. A magenta “DEMO” capsule at the top centre shows the demo state — nothing moves down to make room for it. In landscape and on the iPad it sits in a narrow band above the tabs. You end it under Tools → Help & demo, or in the band with a tap on the capsule; when it ends, all demo data is deleted again. An open point card on the main map closes when the demo starts and when it ends: boat and position change, and its bearing and distance would otherwise still apply to the old boat. When the demo starts, the map jumps once to the demo boat, wherever your iPhone happens to be — if you start it under Tools, the map is there the next time you look at it. After that you pan and zoom freely; the location button brings you back to the boat. The demo start does not ask about notifications. The question only comes when a feature needs it: when recording a voyage of your own, for the anchor alarm (in the demo too), when creating a race or for workshop reminders.

If a recording of your own voyage is running, the demo does not start – the app says so and asks you to end the recording first. Otherwise simulated positions would end up in the real voyage.

The same applies while the anchor alarm or an MOB alarm is running: the app names the button you end it with — “Anchor up” on the map or in the logbook, “End” in the MOB banner on the map. Otherwise the anchor watch would measure against the demo area and report dragging that isn’t happening. An MOB that you try out in the running demo does not stop it. It ends with the demo — even if you set it up again via “Regatta demo” —, without an “MOB ended” entry; its trigger entry goes with the demo data. If the trigger entry lands in one of your own voyages instead of the demo voyage, it stays there, and the end of the demo adds “MOB ended” with the note that the alarm was triggered in the demo — otherwise your logbook would show an open MOB. If the app is closed during the demo, this closing entry is missing. It does not block the next demo start. The block only applies to an MOB that came about without a running demo — that includes one from the demo’s pause (below). Up to Build 34, a demo MOB stayed after the demo ended, with its bearing into the demo area, until you ended it.

The race in it is not a computed one: it is the Wednesday race of the Neustädter Segler-Verein, actually sailed, with 1600 track points at 5-second intervals, the nine course marks of the club course, a kangaroo start and wind between 4 and 16 kn with real shifts. An analysis without dips and mistakes doesn’t show what it is there for — so what you see there is a race that someone actually sailed.

A quick look at another app does not end the demo: it pauses in the background and carries on when you come back. If the app stays away for more than a minute or is closed, the demo ends and clears its data. Etmar lifts an anchor set in the demo in the process — as soon as the app goes into the background, when the demo ends and after a restart. Otherwise the watch would then compare the iPhone’s real position against the point in the demo area: “Anchor dragging” without a real anchor. An MOB triggered in the demo, on the other hand, is left in place by the pause — you find it again when you come back. It only ends with the demo; if the app is closed in the meantime, it is gone at the next launch.

Demo mode is the quickest way through the app. If you have no onboard electronics, you still get to see every Pro feature in action this way.

2. Add your own boat

On a fresh install — also after deleting the app or on a second device — it is preceded by the “Your data” page with two cards: Start fresh leads through the steps below, Continue from iCloud skips boat and sails and fetches your boats from iCloud after a restart (box “When updating” in A2). Below it is the demo button “Let me look around first — start the demo”.

On first launch an onboarding leads you through five steps (including the profile — see below): Boat (only name, boat type and photo — you import the polar and ORC certificate later under Tools → Boat → Polar data), Sails (tap the chips: Main, Jib, Genoa, Spinnaker, Gennaker, Code Zero), Location (see below; there also the “Back up to iCloud” switch with one sentence on what it means) and Offline area (drag the map section, level of detail fixed at “Medium” — other levels under Settings → Map → Offline maps —, the download carries on in the background; below it the question “Load missing areas online” — visible together with its explanation line at the standard text size without scrolling, because the map preview adapts its height to the screen; below that, likewise without scrolling, “Fetch wind from open sources” — suggested on; off means wind only from the onboard network or entered by hand). After that you land in the logbook with the large Cast off button — together with Anchor and MOB fixed at the bottom in the thumb zone, as later on too.

Between Boat and Sails there is a fifth step, Profile: “What do you use Etmar for?” with three large areas — Racing & training, Cruising, Workshop —, all preselected. A tap on an area deselects or reselects it; a tick or an empty circle shows what applies. Deselecting all three is possible — Logbook, Map and Safety always remain. “Next” applies the choice; you can change it at any time under Settings → Profile (C16). With “Continue from iCloud” this step does not appear: the profile applies per device, and out of the box everything is on.

3. Location permission

The question comes exactly once, in the onboarding — on land, with both hands free. For recording in the background you will later also need “Always”; “Cast off” asks for it then. If you chose “Later”, you can catch up on the map (location button or the blue chip “Allow location”). Without location permission the app records nothing and the MOB button has no coordinates.

Test Does the onboarding come across clearly? After the steps, do you feel ready to get going — or is something missing before you cast off for the first time? Is it immediately clear on the “Your data” page which card applies to you? At the “Profile” step, do you know what you are deselecting — and what you would miss afterwards?
↑ Back to contents

A4Pro in the beta: try it without paying

In the beta you can try Pro freely. Pro comes as a subscription; in the TestFlight version the purchase goes through Apple’s test environment, and nothing is charged.

Etmar has two tiers: Free and Pro (what sits where is in B3). Logbook, map, safety and instruments are free; Pro is the analysis afterwards and, on the onboard network, the sailing compass. The subscription is available yearly (€24.99 with a 14-day free trial) or monthly (€4.99). In the TestFlight version the purchase goes through Apple’s test environment, the sandbox: the same path as in the App Store, just without a charge. You can also try Pro without buying: with your own boat via the beta tier, with sample data via the demo tier.

What you see when buying A locked analysis opens “Etmar Pro”: at the top the feature you came from, below it what Pro calculates from GPS and what is added with wind from the onboard network, and at the bottom both subscriptions with prices, restore and the links to the privacy policy and terms of use. Without a running demo, “Try in demo mode” appears above them: the sheet closes, then the demo starts — or the app says why not, for example while a voyage of your own is being recorded. Under Settings → Tier & demo there is “Unlock Pro”, or “Manage subscription” with an active subscription.

With your own boat: the beta tier

Only in the TestFlight version, under Tools → Settings → Tier & demo, is there the section “TestFlight beta” with the Beta tier: Pro or Free. Out of the box it is set to Free — the way the App Store delivers the app. Choose Pro, and the tier lines are gone, without a purchase; the “Tier” line then shows Pro. Below it is the build, so you can mention it in your feedback.

With sample data: the demo tier

If you just want to see quickly how Free or Pro feels, use the demo: Tools → Help & demo → Start demo, then set the Demo tier to Pro or Free. The app behaves like the chosen tier — with the collected tier lines, the Pro overview and simulated onboard instruments, which run in both tiers. The demo tier never lowers you below the tier you have: if the beta tier is set to Pro, the demo stays at Pro too. For everything with your own onboard electronics and your own voyages you need the beta tier instead, because the demo only runs with sample data.

Test Open “Etmar Pro” from a locked feature, for example under Tools → Analyses: is the right feature at the top, and are both subscriptions listed at the bottom with prices? Buy the yearly subscription in the sandbox, nothing is charged: does the sheet close, is the feature unlocked, does Settings → Tier & demo now show “Manage subscription”? The same with restoring on a second device. Without buying: tap “Try in demo mode”: is the sheet closed before the demo rebuilds the interface? End the demo again and under “TestFlight beta” set the beta tier to Pro: are the tier lines gone?
↑ Back to contents

A5What I’m looking at this time

The points from the current build. Everything else is welcome all the same.

(from the next build) Build 59 — anchor watch, depth and wind alarm temporarily unavailable

  • No anchor watch (C13): The anchor alarm, depth alarm and wind alarm are hidden — no “Anchor” in the logbook, no “Set anchor” in the map’s layers menu, no “Anchoring” under Tools → Safety, no anchor tile among the instrument fields. An anchor set before the update is lifted afterwards; anchorages and logbook entries remain. Can you still find any way to the anchor watch?
  • Safety notice again: After the update, “Alarms are aids” appears once more and names only the AIS warning and MOB. Does it appear exactly once?

(from the next build) Build 58 — depths as ready-made files

  • Reload depths (C3): Save an area again, or reload the depths of a large area with the arrow in the pack row under Settings → Map → Offline maps. Are the depth contours where you expect them, and does the area line show contours, quality and source?
  • Very large extent (C3): Use “Load seamarks & depths only” for a very large extent. The area line says how many megabytes the depths would be; the app loads the seamarks independently of that. Do you know what to do next?

Build 56 — safety notice on the alarms

  • New notice at launch: after the update, the sheet “Alarms are aids” appears once; on a new install, directly after the chart notice (“Notice 2 of 2”). Does it appear exactly once, can you read it without scrolling, and is the same text under Settings → About & legal?
  • Limits on the alarms: in the anchor sheet, at the depth alarm and at the AIS warning there is one line each on the limit — no alarm with a flat battery, depth below the transducer, only vessels with AIS. Does that make clear to you what the depth on your depth sounder is measured from?

Build 55 — Pro can be bought

  • Buying Pro (A4, B3): open a locked analysis under Tools → Analyses. Both subscriptions are listed at the bottom with prices; in TestFlight the purchase goes through the sandbox, and nothing is charged. Are price and term right, does the sheet close after the purchase, and is the feature unlocked afterwards?
  • What now belongs to Pro (B3): sailing compass, your own instrument pages, smart fields, the regatta page, several open workshop projects side by side and the planned practice course. Locks only appear in harbour, never on deck. Do you find a lock on a live screen, or a Pro feature that you can use without Pro?

Build 54 — the state for the App Store

  • Pro preview and Tier & demo (B3): open a locked analysis under Tools → Analyses. At the bottom it says “This analysis is part of Pro. Pro is not available in this version.”, and the same line appears under Settings → Tier & demo. Is there still a sentence about a later update, a price or a buy button anywhere?
  • Practice course (C8): under “Course type” there is only windward-leeward. Are you missing something there, or is the sheet clearer this way?

Build 53 — your season against the previous one, plan the practice course in advance, equipment away, English revised

  • Your season (C10, C17): open Tools → Analyses → Your season. At the top you choose the year; below it are seven lines covering all your own sessions: nautical miles, sessions, days underway, longest stage, night hours, harbours & anchorages and the wind profile. Without a subscription, “This season against last” counts both years; Pro sets them value against value, with the wind of both years above; “Share” creates the season PDF. Do the miles, days and harbours match your summer — and does an empty line say why?
  • Plan the practice course in advance Pro (C8, C15): in the Calendar, a training event on your own boat carries “Plan practice course …”: a tap on the map sets the course centre, the axis comes from the forecast for the time of the event, and the app checks depth and shore at the planned location. When creating a training session, the same works with the wind from now. In the training session the tile is then called “Planned course”: the preview places the course at the planned location, takes the axis from the current wind and gives distance and bearing; “Bring to the boat” moves it to the boat. Is the course where you tapped — and does the preview say whether the axis has shifted since the plan?
  • Away (C10, C1): under Tools → Boat → Equipment, open an item and, under “Maintenance”, use “Send away …” to send it for servicing or lend it — to whom, since when; a sail goes away the same way under “Where is it”. The checklist before casting off names what is still missing; “Back” brings it back with one tap and, after servicing, adds the entry to the maintenance log. In the demo the liferaft is at “Seaservice Kiel”. Can you see before casting off that the liferaft is not on board?
  • English revised (D2): set the iPhone to English and go through the logbook, training and analyses. Terms and abbreviations have been unified (for example “underway”, “avg”, “mark”); labels that wrapped or overlapped — steering chart, course briefing, season comparison, quick tiles — are readable again in both languages. Where is there still a German word, a truncated label or a term that an English-speaking sailor would put differently?

Build 47 — the course briefing on one screen, entry points where you look for them

  • “New in Build 47” (C16): after the update, the line “New in Build 47 — take a look” appears in the logbook in harbour. A tap opens “What’s new”, and after that it is gone; without a tap it goes after a week. Did you see it before you went looking for something else?
  • Course briefing (C6, C5): tap a course under Tools → Courses & waypoints, or “Wind against the course” in the race detail. It opens with the map, and the sketch is one tap away (“Map · Sketch”) — north up as on the map, the wind as an arrow; for each leg the table gives the true course — on upwind and downwind legs both tacks, the long one first — and the distance sailed with “≈”. Try “Now”, “At start time” and “Assumed wind”. Tap a leg in the sketch; the button next to “Sketch” opens it in full screen — a double tap zooms, ±10° turns the assumed wind. Do course and distance match what you sail on your course? Can you read the sketch at a glance, including the wind direction? Can you hit the leg with your thumb?
  • Entry points: in the context tab “Voyage”, “Race analysis” appears after a race finishes, and “Measured runs” in training (C6, C8). Under Tools → Prepare, “Offline area” names your saved area (C3). In the sheet of the “Wind now” capsule you switch the wind layer under “On the map” (C3). In Settings → NMEA connection, “Check instruments” opens the check for each sensor (C11). At the top of the trim list, “Workbench” leads straight into the workbench (C9). Do you find each of them at the moment you need it — and is an entry point missing that you would have expected?
  • AIS approach without own course (C4): with GPS on the onboard network, in harbour or at low speed, some sensors leave the course field empty. If the course is older than 10 seconds, the warning calculates with the iPhone’s — if that is also older than 10 seconds, with a stationary boat —, and in the AIS target’s card “without own course” appears after CPA/TCPA. Does a vessel heading towards you still trigger a warning then? Do you understand what the addition means?

Also in Build 47 — your data stays with you, onboard network and map calmer

  • First launch asks about wind (A3): on a new install, at the “Your area offline” step, the app asks whether it may fetch wind from open sources when there is no wind sensor; on is suggested. If you already use the app, you keep your setting. Do you understand without the handbook what you are allowing there?
  • Place names, backup, import (C16): the app looks up the place name when casting off and for the berth using a position rounded to about 1 km. The data backup also shows the state before each restore, with size and “Delete”. The app only accepts GPX, Etmar shares and polar CSV with valid values and says how much it left out. Is the place name still right after casting off? Can you find the state before the restore?
  • Onboard network (C11): over UDP the app only reads the first device after connecting; a second sender shows up as a notice in the connection. The air pressure from the onboard barometer is valid for 10 minutes after the last value. If an NMEA 0183 sensor leaves the course field empty at low speed, the tile greys out the course. Does the air pressure still jump to the iPhone during a short Wi-Fi gap? Does the course grey out in harbour when the sensor stops sending one?
  • Calmer map (C3): there is now only the Contrast map style; the lighthouse menu is shorter, and voyages and races are grouped together. The wind layer only colours from 17 kn, stations carry an arrow as long as the model’s and recognisable by the dot at the end, and a tap on a station in a harbour opens the station instead of the harbour. Do track, course and laylines stand out? Does the menu fit entirely on screen in portrait?
  • Speed by point of sail* Pro (C10): after a session without wind from the onboard network, the app fetches the model wind for every hour; the new card under Tools → Analyses → Boat & speed gives the speed over ground by point of sail* and wind band. Does that match what your boat does on those points of sail?
  • Race on the event (C5): if you create a race in the form with an event, it is attached to that event — the calendar no longer offers a second “Start” for it. The race in the regatta demo is called “NSV Wednesday race (demo)”. Does the calendar still offer you a second “Start” anywhere?

Build 46 — air pressure without an onboard barometer

  • Air pressure from the iPhone in the logbook (C1, C11): without a barometer on the onboard network, create a new logbook entry. iOS asks for “Motion & Fitness” at this point – only here, not when the app starts. Once you allow it, an air pressure marked “iPhone” appears in the field shortly afterwards. Leave the app open for a good hour or record a session and then create a new entry again: under “More”, the “Tendency” line now gives a word and a number. If you decline the request, the field stays empty, and the line below the Weather section says where to switch it on (Settings → Etmar → Motion & Fitness). Does the request come at the right moment? Does the tendency after an hour match what the sky and wind are doing – and a barometer on board, if you have one?

Build 44 — wind for sessions without a wind sensor

  • Open “Collect data” in training, sail a manoeuvre series (C8): Create a training session, tap “Collect data” between two runs and start the manoeuvre series. Can you read the target above “End run” at a glance in a seaway? Does the title of a wind row (“Polar: upwind below 8 kn”) match what your boat is really missing?
  • Session without a wind sensor, then open with a connection (C1, C17): Record a session without wind from the NMEA network, end it and then open the app with a connection. Shortly afterwards, does the session’s daily report show “Wind from station” with the station and distance under every hour, or “Model”? Does the wind roughly match what you had out there, and is it the station you would have expected? Without a connection, or with “Wind from open sources” off, nothing may be fetched.
  • Wind layer for planning (C3): On the map, switch on “Wind (model)” in the lighthouse menu and zoom out until arrows with knots appear; tap an arrow and a station. Under “Wind” there are now two switches, “Model · from ≈ 30 km” and “Stations · every zoom level”: switch both on, zoom right in to the harbour (stations in view stay, from 20 minutes old with their age, the model arrows go) and then far out until the line under “Wind now” says “Arrows every ≈ … km”; finally switch “Wind from open sources” off once. Does the picture help you plan, can you read the figures at a glance — in night mode and zoomed far out too —, or is something missing from it?

Build 43 — two tiers instead of three: everything on board is free, Pro is the analysis afterwards (NMEA, AIS, routes, instrument pages, depth alarm and the facts of a race are free; the Plus tier no longer exists). Switching tier in the beta: Pro · Free — under Tools → Settings → Tier & demo, section “TestFlight beta” (A4). Test as Free first, then as Pro.

  • Free on board without a notice (B3, C11): Map, routes, AIS with CPA warning, laylines, offline packages, dashboard with the standard pages (custom pages and sailing compass with Pro, locked only in the page manager), NMEA connection, pre-start with line bias and the live values in the race no longer carry a tier badge or a lock. If data from the onboard network is missing, the tile says “needs NMEA”. Do you still find “Plus”, “Pro” or a lock anywhere on board?
  • After the race: facts free, one Pro banner (C7): Without Pro, the race analysis shows the key figures and the chapters start, course, manoeuvres and scoring with the facts — mark times, legs, tacks and gybes, legs sailed, SOG profile, zones, course change; distance and crossing at the gun are free in the race detail at the top under “Analysis”. What Pro assesses is gathered in a single collapsed row at the end of the list: “6 more analyses with Pro”. Are the facts enough for you to see where the race tipped — and does the one row make you curious without getting in the way?
  • Pro preview (B3): A row of the collapsed group opens “Etmar Pro” — what Pro calculates from GPS and what is added with wind from the onboard network, without a price or buy button. After this sheet, do you understand what you would get with Pro — and that you need none of it on board?
  • Pro without a wind sensor (C7): With the beta tier Pro the analysis calculates from GPS; what needs wind from the onboard network says so with the data reason, for example “Needs wind from the onboard network at the track points.” With Pro, do you still see a tier notice anywhere instead of a data reason?

Build 42 — an MOB point from a late fix is called “approximate”, “ANCHOR DRAGGING” repeats even without movement, on the watch only holding takes back a sync; plus round courses with either direction in the course editor and a profile that lets you hide what you don’t need (safety stays free; nothing moves between the tiers). Keep testing as Free (A4).

  • MOB point approximate (C13): If the last position is old when MOB is triggered, a precise fix replaces the point only if it is closer to the trigger than the old one — and “Refining position” only shows for as long as that is still possible (three minutes at most). If the new fix comes more than ten seconds after the trigger (on the watch: after the press), the banner permanently says “Point: fix 2 min after the tap”, the radio card “position approximate”. iPhone in your pocket for three minutes, MOB on the watch, wait half a minute, then take the iPhone out: does the banner say “Point refined” (then the location came while still in the pocket) or “Point: fix … after the tap” — and does that time match the time between the press and taking it out?
  • Anchor dragging, no depth (C13): If the anchor drags and the boat then holds again further out, “ANCHOR DRAGGING” comes again every 60 seconds — notification, vibration, sound —, even if it is no longer moving. With a depth sounder: if the sounding drops out during “SHALLOW WATER”, the anchor banner says “No depth — the depth alarm is not watching”, the logbook tile “No depth”; “Depth alarm off” in the anchor sheet can be undone for six seconds. Do you notice in your bunk that the anchor is still lying out — or that the depth alarm is not watching right now?
  • Hold instead of tap on the watch (C5): After “Sync” on the Apple Watch a sync bar shows for six seconds, with “Hold: undo” in small type below. A tap is another sync; only holding for half a second takes back the last one, with its own tap on your wrist. Ideally with gloves: does the hold work, and can you feel the difference from the sync?
  • Postpone the start collapsed (C5): Every pre-start begins with “Postpone the start” collapsed, even if the row with ±5 min was open in the race before. Regatta with two races, expand “Postpone the start” in the first: does the second pre-start begin collapsed, with pin, boat and sync in view?
  • Build a round course (C6): Under Tools → Courses & waypoints, build a course around an island, under “List” in the “Round course” section switch on “Finish = start line” and “Either direction”, and start a race from it. Before the start, does the question “Which direction?” come up, and does it name the right first mark? To try it out without your own course: the demo template “Round Fehmarn”.
  • Change direction (C6): After the start, change the direction via Regatta tab → “Course” → “List” → “Change direction”. Does the race bar then show the right side at every mark, including one whose rounding side you set by hand?
  • Choose a profile, switch areas off, demo (B1, A3): After a fresh install, onboarding asks after the boat “What do you use Etmar for?” — Racing & training, Cruising, Workshop, all preselected. Whatever you switch off loses its entry points: the tile is missing in Tools, and right at the bottom it says “Hidden in your profile: … — Settings › Profile”; what already exists and what is running stays. The demo shows everything, after that your profile applies again. After switching off, is something missing that you still need — or does something stay that you wanted gone?

Known: If the iPhone has discarded a watch sync, the watch still shows the sync bar — holding then does nothing on the iPhone. The “Anchoring” tile under Tools still only says “ANCHOR DRAGGING” when the watch is blind after dragging.

Build 41 — the MOB point says how old it is, shallow water at anchor is visible, the anchor is not dropped on an approximate position; plus undo on the watch and calmer Tools (safety stays free; nothing moves between the tiers). Keep testing as Free (A4).

  • MOB point provisional (C13): If the last position is older than ten seconds when MOB is triggered, the MOB banner shows “Refining position” in orange with the age of the fix. If a precise fix (up to 50 m) comes within three minutes, the point moves there, and “Point refined” shows for 30 seconds; if none comes, the caveat stays. “Point refined” now only for a fix up to ten seconds after the trigger; later the point is called “approximate” — see “MOB point approximate” above. An MOB from the watch requests a location itself for this. App in the background for a while, open it and tap MOB straight away — or MOB on the watch with the iPhone locked in your pocket: can you see at a glance how reliable the point is?
  • Shallow water at anchor (C13): If the depth alarm goes off while the anchor is down, the anchor banner turns red and says “SHALLOW WATER” with sounding and threshold, tile and map chip say it in one word; anchor screen and anchor sheet carry “Depth alarm off” at the top. With a depth sounder: set the threshold in the anchor sheet above the measured depth — can you tell in your bunk that it is the depth and not the anchor?
  • Anchor without precise location (C13): If “Precise Location” is off for Etmar, “Set anchor” no longer sets an anchor — not even if an approximate position is available. At the bottom it says in orange ““Precise Location” off — anchor not set”, a tap on it opens Settings; “Anchor to current position” then does not move the circle either. Switch “Precise Location” off, then “Set anchor” once each from the logbook, the map’s layers menu and Tools → Anchoring: do you find your way back to the switch?
  • Anchor dragging, watch blind (C13): If the anchor drags and no GPS comes after that, the notification says “ANCHOR DRAGGING — WATCH BLIND” with the last distance; the anchor tile in the logbook carries “Watch blind”, the map chip the age of the fix, the anchor screen the reason. If it happens to you: did you know straight away that the boat was last lying out?
  • −5 min before the pre-start (C5): If “−5 min” would bring the gun so close that the pre-start begins immediately, the race stays in “Waiting for pre-start”, and at the bottom “−5 min would start the pre-start — tap “Sync” at the signal” shows for six seconds. Race shortly before the pre-start, “−5 min”: does everything stay as it was?
  • Undo on the watch (C5): After “Sync” on the Apple Watch, “Undo” shows across the full width for six seconds; a tap takes back exactly this sync. Now only undone by holding — see “Hold instead of tap on the watch” above. After the tap, does the countdown jump back on watch and iPhone?
  • NMEA without a gateway (C11): With no reachable gateway, the app retries at growing intervals up to once a minute instead of every few seconds; immediately when you open the instruments and when you return to the app. Gateway off, back on after a few minutes: is the app connected after a little over a minute at the latest — or straight away when you open the instruments?
  • Tools in one colour (B1): The tiles are single-coloured with a thin edge, symbol and title at the top left; colour only shows danger and state — SOS always red, Anchoring depending on the watch, “Help & demo” while the demo is running, “Routes” while a route is running; the chosen focus gets a border. In sunshine and at night: do you find each tile as quickly as before?
  • Entry colours (C1): Entries carry colour only where it means something — cast off and start green, arrival amber, course mark yellow, red only MOB; position, event, radio, tack and gybe are grey, in the list as well as as a pin on the voyage map. Race bar, countdown and Regatta tab show straight digits instead of rounded type. Do you miss a colour — and for what?

Known: If the iPhone has discarded a watch sync, the watch still shows “Undo” — the tap then does nothing. The “Anchoring” tile under Tools only says “ANCHOR DRAGGING” when the watch is blind after dragging.

Build 39 — lighter while underway, the tracking diagnostics record when the app hangs, plus fixes to MOB, anchor watch and recording (in every tier; nothing moves between the tiers). Keep testing as Free (A4).

  • Underway (C1, B2): The logbook no longer recalculates everything on every position update, and logbook, Tools and Analyses no longer do so on every saved track point; the map only moves the boat, course vector and bearings along (not yet with AIS targets or laylines), and “All voyages” loads the tracks in the background — the miles and shape of a row may arrive a moment after the row itself. With recording running, at least 20 minutes: switch tabs, scroll “All voyages” to the end, open the longest voyage including its map — does anything stall for longer than the blink of an eye, and where?
  • Share tracking diagnostics (D3): If the app does not respond for 0.25 seconds or longer, it then writes a line with duration and tab to the log; “Measured hangs since launch” keeps count. Straight after the session, Settings → Tracking diagnostics → “Share diagnostics log” and send it along: does it match what you felt?
  • Reports from iOS (D3): iOS writes metrics about the app at most once a day; “Share reports from iOS” hands them over as a zip file. A day later: is there at least one report — or still “none yet”?
  • Old voyage while recording (C1): Viewing another voyage no longer ends or moves the recording; only the voyage that is recording shows Auto-Tracking “on”. Underway, open and close a completed voyage: does the track have a gap afterwards?
  • MOB without a GPS fix on an old voyage (C13): On the map of a completed voyage, without a fix there is no longer an own boat at the end of the track; an MOB shows “NO POSITION — no fix” and gets its point with the first fix that comes within three minutes — instead of landing at the old end of the track; after that it stays empty instead of marking the boat. In the harbour, location for Etmar set to “Never”, old voyage → map → MOB: is it clear that the point is still missing?
  • Anchor watch blind (C13): With the app open, no more iOS banner as long as the anchor banner, the anchor tile in the logbook or the anchor screen is visible and no sheet is on top; “ANCHOR DRAGGING” remains a banner. The “Anchor set” tile is labelled in dark green by day. Can you see the state of the watch without a banner covering the header?

Known: With AIS targets in range, laylines to the Go-To target (Pro with a wind sensor), during the MOB alarm and while the map follows the boat, the map still recalculates on every position update. In the demo the recording is on the demo race, which is why the demo boat’s day sail shows the Auto-Tracking switch as off.
